, it suffices to show that the probability measures agree on the product of disjoint partitions.
Let
\{B_{k,j}\mid j\in J_{k}\}=\mathsf{ev}(\mathcal{Q}_{k})
for
k\in\{1,2\}
. For any
i\in J_{1}
and
j\in J_{2}
, we have:

\displaystyle\nu(B_{1,i}\ast B_{2,j}\ast\mathcal{I})

\displaystyle={\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu)}}\mu(\sigma)\cdot\nu_{\sigma}(B_{1,i}\ast B_{2,j}\ast\mathcal{I})

Instead of summing over the support of
\sigma
, we can alternatively sum over the elements of the
A_{1,i^{\prime}}
and
A_{2,j^{\prime}}
sets.

\displaystyle=\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I_{1}}\sum_{j^{\prime}\in I_{2}}\sum_{\sigma_{1}\in A_{1,i^{\prime}}}\sum_{\sigma_{2}\in A_{2,j^{\prime}}}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{I}}\mu(\sigma_{1}\uplus\sigma_{2}\uplus\tau)\cdot\nu_{\sigma}(B_{1,i}\ast B_{2,j}\ast\mathcal{I})

We previously showed that
\nu_{k}(B_{k,\ell}\ast\mathcal{I})=p_{B_{k,\ell},k,i^{\prime}}
(a constant) for any
\nu_{k}\in\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k})(\sigma)
where
\sigma\in A_{k,i^{\prime}}
. So, we can use
Lemma

C.5
to conclude that:

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I_{1}}\sum_{j^{\prime}\in I_{2}}\sum_{\sigma_{1}\in A_{1,i^{\prime}}}\sum_{\sigma_{2}\in A_{2,j^{\prime}}}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{I}}\mu(\sigma_{1}\uplus\sigma_{2}\uplus\tau)\cdot p_{B_{1,i},1,i^{\prime}}\cdot p_{B_{2,j},2,j^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I_{1}}\sum_{j^{\prime}\in I_{2}}\mu(A_{1,i^{\prime}}\ast A_{2,j^{\prime}}\ast\mathcal{I})\cdot p_{B_{1,i},1,i^{\prime}}\cdot p_{B_{2,j},2,j^{\prime}}

By
Equation

5
.

\displaystyle=\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I_{1}}\sum_{j^{\prime}\in I_{2}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{I}}}(A_{1,i^{\prime}}\ast\mathcal{I})\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{I}}}(A_{2,j^{\prime}}\ast\mathcal{I})\cdot p_{B_{1,i},1,i^{\prime}}\cdot p_{B_{2,j},2,j^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I_{1}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{I}}}(A_{1,i^{\prime}}\ast\mathcal{I})\cdot p_{B_{1,i},1,i^{\prime}}\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{j^{\prime}\in I_{2}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{I}}}(A_{2,j^{\prime}}\ast\mathcal{I})\cdot p_{B_{2,j},2,j^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mu_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{I}}}(B_{1,i}\ast\mathcal{I})\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{I}}}(B_{2,j}\ast\mathcal{I})

\displaystyle=\mu_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(B_{1,i})\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}(B_{2,j})\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{I}}}(\mathcal{I})

Now, we have shown that
\nu(B_{1,i}\ast B_{2,j}\ast\mathcal{I})\geq\mu_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{Q}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{I}}}(B_{1,i}\ast B_{2,j}\ast\mathcal{I})
for all
i\in J_{1}
and
j\in J_{2}
. Since
\sum_{i\in J_{1}}\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\mu_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}\ast\mathcal{Q}_{2}}(B_{1,i}\ast B_{2,j})=1
, then
\nu(B_{1,i}\ast B_{2,j}\ast\mathcal{I})
cannot be strictly greater then
\mu_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(B_{1,i})\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}(B_{2,j})
for any
i
or
j
, and therefore the quantities must be equal.

∎

Appendix D
Almost Sure Termination

For any
S\in\mathcal{C}(\mathsf{Mem}[V])
, let
\mathsf{minterm}(S)=\mathsf{minProb}(S,\mathsf{Mem}[V])
,
i.e.,
it is the minimum probability that the program terminates. Also, let
\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}\colon(\mathsf{Mem}[V]\to\mathcal{C}(\mathsf{Mem}[V]))\to\mathsf{Mem}[V]\to\mathcal{C}(\mathsf{Mem}[V])
be defined as follows:

\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}(f)(\sigma)\triangleq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}f^{\dagger}(\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)(\sigma))&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Test}}(\sigma)=\mathsf{true}\\
\eta(\sigma)&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Test}}(\sigma)=\mathsf{false}\end{array}\right.

By
Zilberstein
et al. (2025a, Lemma 5.2)
:

\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}(\left\llbracket\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C\right\rrbracket)=\mathsf{lfp}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}\right)=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Psi^{n}_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet})

Now, for any test
b
and distribution
\mu
we define a conditioning operator as follows:

(b\textsf{?}\mu)(\sigma)\triangleq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{\mu(\sigma)}{\mu(b)}&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Test}}(\sigma)=\mathsf{true}\\
0&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Test}}(\sigma)=\mathsf{false}\end{array}\right.\hskip 17.00024pt\text{where}\hskip 17.00024pt\mu(b)\triangleq\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu)\mid\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Test}}(\sigma)=\mathsf{true}}\mu(\sigma)

Note that if
\mu(b)=0
, then
b\textsf{?}\mu
is not well-defined. In that case, we just let
b\textsf{?}\mu=\bot_{\mathcal{D}}
. It is also clearly true that
\mu=(b\textsf{?}\mu)\oplus_{\mu(b)}(\lnot b\textsf{?}\mu)
for any
b
and
\mu
. In addition, we call
\langle\varphi,\psi\rangle
an loop invariant pair for
\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C
under the resource invariant
I
iff:

(1)

\varphi\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b\mapsto\mathsf{true}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil

(2)

\psi\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b\mapsto\mathsf{false}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil

(3)

I\vDash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi\mathbin{\&}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}

(4)

\mathsf{precise}(\psi)

Given these new definitions, we prove some partial correctness results, which show that the
\psi
(as defined above) holds on the terminating portion of the result of a while loop.

Lemma D.1.

Take any
\Gamma
, let
\mathcal{I}=\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}
,
\langle\varphi,\psi\rangle
be an invariant pair for
\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C
under
I
, and
\mathcal{Q}
be the unique smallest probability space satisfying
\psi
(which exists since
\mathsf{precise}(\psi)
).
For any
\mu
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
A\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}
such that
\Gamma,\mu\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
:

\mathsf{minProb}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu),A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)=\mathsf{minterm}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu)\right)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(A)

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
n
. Suppose that
n=0
, and so we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{minProb}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{0}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu),A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{minProb}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}},A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle=\mathsf{minterm}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}\right)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(A)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{minterm}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{0}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu)\right)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(A)

Now suppose that
n=1
, and so we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{minProb}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{1}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu),A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{minProb}\left(\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)\right),A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{minProb}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}},A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle=\mathsf{minterm}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}\right)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(A)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{minterm}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{1}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu)\right)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(A)

Now suppose that
n>1
. Then, we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{minProb}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu),A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{minProb}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n-1}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)),A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)

\displaystyle=\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)}\mathsf{minProb}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n-1}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(\nu),A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)

Since
\langle\varphi,\psi\rangle
is an invariant pair and
\Gamma,\mu\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, then every such
\nu
above can be split into
\nu=b\textsf{?}\nu\oplus_{\nu(b)}\lnot b\textsf{?}\nu
such that
\Gamma,b\textsf{?}\nu\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
and
\Gamma,\lnot b\textsf{?}\nu\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.

\displaystyle=\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)}\mathsf{minProb}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n-1}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(b\textsf{?}\nu\oplus_{\nu(b)}\lnot b\textsf{?}\nu),A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)

\displaystyle=\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)}\mathsf{minProb}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n-1}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(b\textsf{?}\nu)\oplus_{\nu(b)}\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n-1}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(\lnot b\textsf{?}\nu),A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)

Since
n>1
, then
\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n-1}(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet})^{\dagger}(\lnot b\textsf{?}\nu)=\{\lnot b\textsf{?}\nu\}
.

\displaystyle=\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)}\mathsf{minProb}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n-1}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(b\textsf{?}\nu)\oplus_{\nu(b)}\{\lnot b\textsf{?}\nu\},A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)

\displaystyle=\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)}\nu(b)\cdot\mathsf{minProb}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n-1}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(b\textsf{?}\nu),A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)+(1-\nu(b))\cdot(\lnot b\textsf{?}\nu)(A\ast\mathcal{I})

Since
\Gamma,\lnot b\textsf{?}\nu\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, then
(\lnot b\textsf{?}\nu)(A\ast\mathcal{I})=\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(A)
. Also using the induction hypothesis, we get:

\displaystyle=\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)}\nu(b)\cdot\mathsf{minterm}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n-1}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(b\textsf{?}\nu)\right)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(A)+(1-\nu(b))\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(A)

\displaystyle=\left(\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)}\mathsf{minterm}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n-1}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(b\textsf{?}\nu)\oplus_{\nu(b)}\{\lnot b\textsf{?}\nu\}\right)\right)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(A)

\displaystyle=\left(\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)}\mathsf{minterm}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n-1}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(\nu)\right)\right)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(A)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{minterm}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu)\right)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(A)

∎

Lemma D.2.

Take any
\Gamma
, let
\mathcal{I}=\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}
,
\langle\varphi,\psi\rangle
be an invariant pair for
\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C
under
I
, and
\mathcal{Q}
be the unique smallest probability space satisfying
\psi
(which exists since
\mathsf{precise}(\psi)
).
For any
\mu
such that
\Gamma,\mu\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
and
A\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}
:

\mathsf{minProb}\left(\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}\left(\left\llbracket\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C\right\rrbracket\right)^{\dagger}(\mu),A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)=\mathsf{minterm}\left(\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}\left(\left\llbracket\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C\right\rrbracket\right)^{\dagger}(\mu)\right)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(A)

Proof.

The proof proceeds as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{minProb}\left(\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}\left(\left\llbracket\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C\right\rrbracket\right)^{\dagger}(\mu),A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{minProb}\left(\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu),A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)

\displaystyle=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{minProb}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu),A\ast\mathcal{I}\right)

By
Lemma

D.1
.

\displaystyle=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{minterm}\left(\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu)\right)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(A)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{minterm}\left(\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Psi_{\langle b,C,\mathcal{I}\rangle}^{n}\left(\bot_{\mathcal{C}}^{\bullet}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu)\right)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(A)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{minterm}\left(\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}(\left\llbracket\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)\right)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(A)

∎

Corollary D.3 (Almost Sure Termination).

Take any
\Gamma
and
0<p\leq 1
, let
\mathcal{I}=\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}
, and
\langle\varphi,\psi\rangle
be an invariant pair for
\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C
under
I
. If additionally
\mathsf{minterm}(\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}(\left\llbracket\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu))\geq p
for all
\mu
such that
\Gamma,\mu\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, then
\mathsf{minterm}(\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{I}}(\left\llbracket\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu))=1
.

Proof.

Follows by an identical argument to Lemma D.3 of
Zilberstein et al. (2025b)
.
∎

Appendix E
Logic and Rules

Lemma E.1 (Monotonicity).

If
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi
and
\mathcal{P}\preceq\mathcal{Q}
, then
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\varphi
.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
\varphi
.

•

\varphi=\top
. Since
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\top
for all
\mathcal{Q}
, the claim holds trivially.

•

\varphi=\bot
. The premise is false, therefore this case is vacuous.

•

\varphi=\varphi_{1}\land\varphi_{2}
. By the induction hypothesis, we know that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\varphi_{1}
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\varphi_{2}
, therefore
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\varphi_{1}\land\varphi_{2}
.

•

\varphi=\varphi_{1}\lor\varphi_{2}
. Without loss of generality, suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi_{1}
. By the induction hypothesis, we know that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\varphi_{1}
, therefore by weakening
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\varphi_{1}\lor\varphi_{2}
.

•

\varphi=\exists X.\psi
. We know that there is some
v\in\mathsf{Val}
such that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathcal{P}\vDash\psi
. By the induction hypothesis, we get that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi
. This implies that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\exists X.\psi
.

•

\varphi=\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\psi
. Immediate, since the semantics stipulates that
\mathcal{P}
is greater than the direct sum, therefore
\mathcal{Q}
is also greater than the direct sum.

•

\varphi=\bignd_{X\in E}\psi
. We know that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bigoplus_{X\sim\mu}
for some
\mu\in\mathcal{D}(\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma))
. By the previous case, we get that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\bigoplus_{X\sim\mu}\psi
. This implies that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\bignd_{X\in E}\psi
.

•

\varphi=\varphi_{1}\ast_{m}\varphi_{2}
. Immediate, since we know that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{2}\preceq\mathcal{P}
such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{i}\vDash\varphi_{i}
for each
i
, and therefore
\mathcal{P}_{1}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{2}\preceq\mathcal{Q}
as well.

the semantics stipulates that
\mathcal{P}
is greater than the independent product, therefore
\mathcal{Q}
is also greater than the independent product.

•

\varphi=\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Let
\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}=\mathsf{Mem}[S]
and
\Omega_{\mathcal{Q}}=\mathsf{Mem}[T]
, and note that
S\subseteq T
since
\mathcal{P}\preceq\mathcal{Q}
. We know that
\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{S}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}
and
\mu_{\mathcal{P}}(\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{S})=1
.
We also know that
\mu_{\mathcal{P}}(\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{S})=\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(\bigcup_{B\mid\pi_{S}(B)=\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{S}}B)=1
. Note that by definition
\bigcup_{B\mid\pi_{S}(B)=\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{S}}B\subseteq\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{T}
. Since that set has probability 1 and
\mathcal{Q}
is a complete probability space, then
\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{T}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}
, and also has probability 1.

∎

E.1.
Precise and Convex Assertions

Lemma E.2.

\mathsf{precise}(\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil)

Proof.

Take any
\Gamma
. If
\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}=\emptyset
, then
P
is unsatisfiable under
\Gamma
, so we are done. If not, then let
\Omega=\mathsf{Mem}[\mathsf{fv}(P)]
,
\mathcal{F}=\{A\subseteq\Omega\mid\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}\subseteq A\}\cup\{A\subseteq\Omega\mid A\cap\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}=\emptyset\}
and:

\mu(A)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\text{if}~\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}\subseteq A\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

It is relatively easy to see that
\mu
is a probability measure since
\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}
is the smallest measurable set with nonzero probability, and it has probability 1, so the countable additivity property holds. By definition,
\Gamma,\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu\rangle\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Clearly it is also minimal, since any other
\mathcal{P}
such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
must also include
\mathcal{F}
as measurable sets by definition, and must assign probability 1 to the event
\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}
.
∎

Lemma E.3.

If
\mathsf{precise}(\varphi,\psi)
, then
\mathsf{precise}(\varphi\ast\psi)
.

Proof.

Take any
\Gamma
, if either
\varphi
or
\psi
is not satisfiable under
\Gamma
, then neither is
\varphi\ast\psi
, and then the claim holds vacuously. If both are satisfiable, then there are unique smallest
\mathcal{P}_{1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}
such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\vDash\varphi
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{2}\vDash\psi
. Clearly, this means that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}\vDash\varphi\ast\psi
. We now argue that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}
is minimal.
Take any
\mathcal{Q}
such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\varphi\ast\psi
. This means that there are
\mathcal{Q}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{Q}_{2}\preceq\mathcal{Q}
such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}_{1}\vDash\varphi
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}_{2}\vDash\psi
. By precision of
\varphi
and
\psi
, we know that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\preceq\mathcal{Q}_{1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}\preceq\mathcal{Q}_{2}
. Using
Lemma

B.4
, we get:

\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}\preceq\mathcal{Q}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{Q}_{2}\preceq\mathcal{Q}

∎

Lemma E.4.

If
\mathsf{precise}(\varphi)
and
\varphi\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e\mapsto X\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, then
\mathsf{precise}(\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi)
.

Proof.

Take any
\Gamma
and let
\nu=d(\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma)
, if
\varphi
is unsatisfiable under any
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v]
, then so is
\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi
, so the claim holds vacuously. If not, then there is a unique smallest
\mathcal{P}_{v}
such that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathcal{P}_{v}\vDash\varphi
for each
v\in\mathsf{supp}(\nu)
. Since
\varphi\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e\mapsto X\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, we can create new disjoint probability spaces
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{v}
, where each
\Omega_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{v}}=\{\sigma\in\Omega_{\mathcal{P}_{v}}\mid\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(\sigma)=v\}
. Note that this does not remove any samples that have positive probability, and clearly
\mathcal{P}_{v}=\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{v})
. Let
\mathcal{P}=\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}\mathcal{P}_{v}^{\prime}
, then
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi
. It only remains to show that
\mathcal{P}
is minimal.

Take any
\mathcal{Q}
such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi
. That means that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{Q}_{v})\vDash\varphi
, where
\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}\mathcal{Q}_{v}\preceq\mathcal{Q}
. Since
\varphi
is precise, then
\mathcal{P}_{v}=\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{v})\preceq\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{Q}_{v})
for each
v
. Since the completion only expands the sample space with zero probability events, this must mean that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{v}\preceq\mathcal{Q}_{v}
as well. Therefore, by
Lemma

B.5
:

\mathcal{P}=\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{v}\preceq\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}\mathcal{Q}_{v}\preceq\mathcal{Q}

∎

Lemma E.5.

If
\mathsf{convex}(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})
, then
\mathsf{convex}(\varphi_{1}\mathop{\ast_{\mathsf{w}}}\varphi_{2})
.

Proof.

Take any
\Gamma
, if
\varphi_{1}\mathop{\ast_{\mathsf{w}}}\varphi_{2}
is satisfiable under
\Gamma
, then so are each
\varphi_{k}
.
Since each
\varphi_{k}
is convex, we know that there exist
\Omega_{k}
,
\mathcal{F}_{k}
, and
S_{k}
such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi
iff
\langle\Omega_{k},\mathcal{F}_{k},\mu\rangle\preceq\mathcal{P}
for some
\mu\in S_{k}
.
Let
U
and
V
be sets such that
\Omega_{1}=\mathsf{Mem}[U]
and
\Omega_{2}=\mathsf{Mem}[V]
, let
\Omega=\Omega_{1}\ast\Omega_{2}
,
\mathcal{F}
be the smallest
\sigma
-algebra containing
\{A\ast B\mid A\in\mathcal{F}_{1},B\in\mathcal{F}_{2}\}
, and
S=\{\mu\mid\pi_{U}(\mu)\in S_{1},\pi_{V}(\mu)\in S_{2}\}
(
S
is clearly convex since
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
are convex).

We complete the proof by showing that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi_{1}\mathop{\ast_{\mathsf{w}}}\varphi_{2}
iff
\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu\rangle\preceq\mathcal{P}
for some
\mu\in S
. For the forward direction, suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi_{1}\mathop{\ast_{\mathsf{w}}}\varphi_{2}
, so
\Gamma,\pi_{U}(\mathcal{P})\vDash\varphi_{1}
and
\Gamma,\pi_{V}(\mathcal{P})\vDash\varphi_{2}
. Due to convexity of
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
, there must be
\mu_{1}\in S_{1}
and
\mu_{2}\in S_{2}
such that
\langle\Omega_{1},\mathcal{F}_{1},\mu_{1}\rangle\preceq\pi_{U}(\mathcal{P})
and
\langle\Omega_{2},\mathcal{F}_{2},\mu_{2}\rangle\preceq\pi_{V}(\mathcal{P})
. Now, let:

\mu(A)\triangleq\mu_{\mathcal{P}}\left(\bigcup\left\{B\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}\mid\pi_{U\cup V}(B)=A\right\}\right)

This gives us:

\pi_{U}(\mu)(A)=\mu(A\ast\mathsf{Mem}[V])=\mu_{\mathcal{P}}\left(\bigcup\left\{B\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}\mid\pi_{U\cup V}(B)=A\ast\mathsf{Mem}[V]\right\}\right)=\mu_{\mathcal{P}}\left(\bigcup\left\{B\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}\mid\pi_{U}(B)=A\right\}\right)=\mu_{1}(A)

And similarly,
\pi_{V}(\mu)(B)=\mu_{2}(B)
, therefore
\mu\in S
by construction, and so also by construction
\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu\rangle\preceq\mathcal{P}
.

For the reverse direction, suppose that
\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu\rangle\preceq\mathcal{P}
for some
\mu\in S
. That means that
\pi_{U}(\mu)\in S_{1}
and
\pi_{V}(\mu)\in S_{2}
, and therefore
\Gamma,\langle\Omega_{1},\mathcal{F}_{1},\pi_{U}(\mu)\rangle\vDash\varphi_{1}
and
\Gamma,\langle\Omega_{2},\mathcal{F}_{2},\pi_{V}(\mu)\rangle\vDash\varphi_{2}
. Since
\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu\rangle\in\langle\Omega_{1},\mathcal{F}_{1},\pi_{U}(\mu)\rangle\diamond_{\mathsf{w}}\langle\Omega_{2},\mathcal{F}_{2},\pi_{V}(\mu)\rangle
, then
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi_{1}\mathop{\ast_{\mathsf{w}}}\varphi_{2}
.
∎

Lemma E.6.

If
\mathsf{convex}(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})
,
\varphi_{1}\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e\mapsto 1\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, and
\varphi_{2}\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e\mapsto 0\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, then
\mathsf{convex}(\varphi_{1}\oplus_{\geq E}\varphi_{2})
.

Proof.

Take any
\Gamma
, if
\varphi_{1}\oplus_{\geq E}\varphi_{2}
is satisfiable under
\Gamma
, then so are
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
.
Let
p=\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma)
and let
X
be the variable that is bound by
\oplus_{\geq E}
.
Since the
\varphi_{k}
are convex, then for each
k\in\{1,2\}
there exist
\Omega_{k}
,
\mathcal{F}_{k}
, and
S_{k}
such that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq 2-k],\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi_{k}
iff
\langle\Omega_{k},\mathcal{F}_{k},\mu\rangle\preceq\mathcal{P}
for some
\mu\in S_{k}
. Now, let:

\Omega\triangleq\Omega_{1}\cup\Omega_{2}\par\mathcal{F}\triangleq\left\{A\subseteq\Omega\mid\{\sigma\in A\mid\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(\sigma)=1\}\in\mathcal{F}_{1},\{\sigma\in A\mid\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(\sigma)=0\}\in\mathcal{F}_{2}\right\}\par S\triangleq\{\mu\oplus_{q}\nu\mid\mu\in S_{1},\nu\in S_{2},p\leq q\leq 1\}\par\text{where}\hskip 8.50012pt(\mu\oplus_{q}\nu)(A)=q\cdot\mu(\{\sigma\in A\mid\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(\sigma)=1\})+(1-q)\cdot\nu(\{\sigma\in A\mid\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(\sigma)=0\})

We complete the proof by showing that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi_{1}\oplus_{\geq E}\varphi_{2}
iff
\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu\rangle\preceq\mathcal{P}
for some
\mu\in S
. For the forward direction, suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi_{1}\oplus_{\geq E}\varphi_{2}
, so there exists a
q\geq p
such that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq 1],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{1})\vDash\varphi_{1}
and
\Gamma[X\coloneqq 0],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{2})\vDash\varphi_{2}
for some
\mathcal{P}_{1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}
such that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\oplus_{q}\mathcal{P}_{2}\preceq\mathcal{P}
. This means that
\langle\Omega_{k},\mathcal{F}_{k},\mu_{k}\rangle\preceq\mathsf{ext}(P_{k})
for some
\mu_{k}\in S_{k}
for each
k\in\{1,2\}
. So, letting
\mu=\mu_{1}\oplus_{q}\mu_{2}
, clearly
\mu\in S
by construction. Finally, we get:

\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu\rangle=\langle\Omega_{1},\mathcal{F}_{1},\mu_{1}\rangle\oplus_{q}\langle\Omega_{2},\mathcal{F}_{2},\mu_{2}\rangle\preceq\mathcal{P}_{1}\oplus_{q}\mathcal{P}_{2}\preceq\mathcal{P}

For the reverse direction, suppose that
\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu\rangle\preceq\mathcal{P}
for some
\mu\in S
.
Since
\mu\in S
, then there exist
\mu_{1}\in S_{1}
,
\mu_{2}\in S_{2}
, and
q\geq p
such that
\mu=\mu_{1}\oplus_{q}\mu_{2}
. We know by construction that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq 2-k],\langle\Omega_{k},\mathcal{F}_{k},\mu_{k}\rangle\vDash\varphi_{k}
for each
k
, therefore clearly
\Gamma,\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu\rangle\vDash\varphi_{1}\oplus_{q}\varphi_{2}
. Therefore, by
Lemma

E.1
, we know that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi_{1}\oplus_{q}\varphi_{2}
. Finally, we weaken this assertion to get
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi_{1}\oplus_{\geq E}\varphi_{2}
.
∎

Lemma E.7.

If
\mathsf{convex}(\varphi)
and
\varphi\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e\mapsto X\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, then
\mathsf{convex}(\bignd_{X\in E}\varphi)
.

Proof.

Take any
\Gamma
, if
\bigoplus_{X\in E}\varphi
is satisfiable under
\Gamma
, then so is
\varphi
. Let
S=\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma)
, so for each
v\in S
, there must be
\Omega_{v}
,
\mathcal{F}_{v}
, and
S_{v}
such that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi
iff
\langle\Omega_{v},\mathcal{F}_{v},\mu\rangle\preceq\mathcal{P}
for some
\mu\in S_{v}
.
Now let:

\Omega\triangleq\bigcup_{v\in S}\Omega_{v}\par\mathcal{F}\triangleq\left\{A\subseteq\Omega\mid\forall v\in S.\ \{\sigma\in A\mid\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(\sigma)=v\}\in\mathcal{F}_{v}\right\}\par T\triangleq\left\{\sum_{v\in S}\xi(v)\cdot\mu_{v}\mid\xi\in\mathcal{D}(S),\forall v.\ \mu_{v}\in S_{v}\right\}

Clearly
T
is convex, since it is constructed as countable convex combinations of convex sets
(
Zilberstein et al., 2025b
, Lemma B.1)
.
We now show that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bignd_{X\in E}\varphi
iff
\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu\rangle\preceq\mathcal{P}
for some
\mu\in T
. For the forward direction, suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bignd_{X\in E}\varphi
, so
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})\vDash\varphi
for each
v\in\mathsf{supp}(\nu)
where
\nu\in\mathcal{D}(S)
and
\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}\mathcal{P}_{v}\preceq\mathcal{P}
. This means that for each
v
, there is a
\mu_{v}
such that
\langle\Omega_{v},\mathcal{F}_{v},\mu_{v}\rangle\preceq\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})
. So, letting
\mu=\sum_{v\in S}\nu(v)\cdot\mu_{v}
, we get that
\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu\rangle\preceq\mathcal{P}
by construction.

For the reverse direction, suppose that
\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu\rangle\preceq\mathcal{P}
for some
\mu\in T
. Since
\mu\in T
, then there exists a
\xi\in\mathcal{D}(S)
and
\mu_{v}\in S_{v}
for all
v\in S
such that
\mu=\sum_{v\in S}\xi(v)\cdot\mu_{v}
. By definition,
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\langle\Omega_{v},\mathcal{F}_{v},\mu_{v}\rangle\vDash\varphi
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{v}
be the restriction of
\langle\Omega_{v},\mathcal{F}_{v},\mu_{v}\rangle
to the states where
\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(\sigma)=v
, then in order to show that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bignd_{X\in E}\varphi
, it will suffice to show that
\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\mathcal{P}_{v}\preceq\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu\rangle
, and then we can conclude that
\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\mathcal{P}_{v}\preceq\mathcal{P}
by transitivity and complete the proof by
Lemma

E.1
. The conditions on
\Omega
and
\mathcal{F}
hold by construction. For the probability measure, we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\mathcal{P}_{v}}(A)

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in S}\xi(v)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{v}}(A\cap\Omega_{v})

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in S}\xi(v)\cdot\mu_{v}(A\cap\Omega_{v})

Since each
\mu_{v}
assigns 0 probability outside of
\Omega_{v}
, we can remove the intersection.

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in S}\xi(v)\cdot\mu_{v}(A)

\displaystyle=\mu(A)

∎

E.2.
Entailment Rules

Lemma E.8.

The entailment rules in
Figure

4
are valid.

Proof.

(1)

\frac{P\vdash Q}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\vdash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil}

Suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, where
\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}=\mathsf{Mem}[S]
. That means that
\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{S}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}
and
\mu(\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{S})=1
. Since
P\vdash Q
, then it must be that
\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{S}\subseteq\llparenthesis Q\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{S}
, therefore
\llparenthesis Q\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{S}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}
because
\mathcal{P}
is a complete probability space and all samples outside of
\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{S}
must have measure 0. By the additivity property of probability measures
\mu(\llparenthesis Q\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{S})=1
.

(2)

\frac{\varphi\vdash\varphi^{\prime}\hskip 8.24454pt\psi\vdash\psi^{\prime}}{\varphi\ast_{m}\psi\vdash\varphi^{\prime}\ast_{m}\psi^{\prime}}

Suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi\ast_{m}\psi
, so
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\vDash\varphi
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{2}\vDash\psi
and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mathcal{P}
for some
\mathcal{P}_{1}
,
\mathcal{P}_{2}
, and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{2}
. Since
\varphi\vdash\varphi^{\prime}
and
\psi\vdash\psi^{\prime}
, then
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\vDash\varphi^{\prime}
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{2}\vDash\psi^{\prime}
, therefore we immediately conclude that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi^{\prime}\ast_{m}\psi^{\prime}
.

(3)

\varphi\ast\psi\vdash\varphi\mathop{\ast_{\mathsf{w}}}\psi

Suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi\ast\psi
, so
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\vDash\varphi
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{2}\vDash\psi
for some
\mathcal{P}_{1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}
such that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}\preceq\mathcal{P}
. Clearly,
\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}\diamond_{\mathsf{w}}\mathcal{P}_{2}
, therefore this immediately implies that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi\mathop{\ast_{\mathsf{w}}}\psi
.

(4)

\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\ast Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\dashv\vdash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast_{m}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil

We first show that
\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\ast Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\vdash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast_{m}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\ast Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, where
\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}=\mathsf{Mem}[S]
. That means that
\llparenthesis P\ast Q\rrparenthesis^{S}_{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}
and
\mu_{\mathcal{P}}(\llparenthesis P\ast Q\rrparenthesis^{S}_{\Gamma})=1
.
Now let
\mathcal{P}_{1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}
be the smallest probability spaces such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{2}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, so clearly
\mathcal{P}_{1}\diamond_{\mathsf{w}}\mathcal{P}_{2}=\mathcal{P}_{1}\diamond_{\mathsf{s}}\mathcal{P}_{2}=\{\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}\}
and therefore
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast_{m}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. It is also clearly the case that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}\preceq\mathcal{P}
since the smallest event with nonzero measure in
\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}
is
\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}\ast\llparenthesis Q\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}
, which has probability 1, therefore everything larger also has probability 1, and it suffices to show that:

\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}}(\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}\ast\llparenthesis Q\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma})=1=\mu_{\mathcal{P}}\left(\llparenthesis P\ast Q\rrparenthesis^{S}_{\Gamma}\right)=\mu_{\mathcal{P}}\left(\textstyle\bigcup_{B\mid\pi_{\mathsf{fv}(P,Q)}(B)=\llparenthesis P\ast Q\rrparenthesis^{S}_{\Gamma}}B\right)

Therefore, by
Lemma

E.1
,
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast_{m}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.

Now we show that
\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast_{m}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\vdash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\ast Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast_{m}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. That means that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mathcal{P}
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{2}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
for some
\mathcal{P}_{1}
,
\mathcal{P}_{2}
, and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{2}
. This also means that
\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}(\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis^{S}_{\Gamma})=1
and
\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{2}}(\llparenthesis Q\rrparenthesis^{T}_{\Gamma})=1
(where
\Omega_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}=\mathsf{Mem}[S]
and
\Omega_{\mathcal{P}_{2}}=\mathsf{Mem}[T]
). Therefore, we know that
\pi_{S}(\mu_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}})(\llparenthesis P\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{S})=1
and
\pi_{T}(\mu_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}})(\llparenthesis Q\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{T})=1
, and therefore we know that anything outside of
P
and
Q
has measure zero, and therefore it must be the case that
\mu_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}(\llparenthesis P\ast Q\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}^{S\cup T})=1
.
So,
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\vDash\llparenthesis P\ast Q\rrparenthesis
, and since
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mathcal{P}
, then by
Lemma

E.1
,
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\llparenthesis P\ast Q\rrparenthesis
.

(5)

\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\dashv\vdash\varphi\mathop{\ast_{\mathsf{w}}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil

The forward direction follows immediately from
Item

3
, so we prove only the reverse direction. Suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi\mathop{\ast_{\mathsf{w}}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. This means that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\vDash\varphi
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{2}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
for some
\mathcal{P}_{1}
,
\mathcal{P}_{2}
, and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}\diamond_{\mathsf{w}}\mathcal{P}_{2}
. Now let
\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}
be the smallest probability space such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.
Since
\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}
contains events only of measure 0 or 1, then
\mathcal{P}_{1}\diamond_{\mathsf{w}}\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}=\{\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}\}
, therefore it must be that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}\preceq\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mathcal{P}
. Therefore, by definition,
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.

(6)

\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi\vdash\bignd_{X\in\mathsf{supp}(d(E))}\varphi

Suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi
, therefore
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})\vDash\varphi
for each
v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)
where
\xi=d(\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma))
. Obviously
\xi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{supp}(\xi))
, so this immediately implies that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bignd_{\mathsf{supp}(d(E))}\varphi
.

(7)

\varphi[E/X]\dashv\vdash\bignd_{X\in\{E\}}\varphi

For the forward direction, suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi[E/X]
. Let
v=\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma)
. We therefore have that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v]\vDash\varphi
. Obviously,
\delta_{v}\in\mathcal{D}(\{v\})
, therefore we get that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bignd_{X\in\{E\}}\varphi
.

For the reverse direction, suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bignd_{X\in\{E\}}\varphi
. Since the only distribution over a singleton support is the point-mass distribution, this immediately gives us
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v]\vDash\varphi
, where again
v=\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma)
. Finally, we conclude that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi[E/X]
.

(8)

\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E\subseteq E^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\bignd_{X\in E}\varphi\vdash\bignd_{X\in E^{\prime}}\varphi

Suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E\subseteq E^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\bignd_{X\in E}\varphi
. Let
S=\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma)
and
S^{\prime}=\left\llbracket E^{\prime}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma)
. We therefore know that
S\subseteq S^{\prime}
and
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})\vDash\varphi
for all
v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)
and some
\xi\in\mathcal{D}(S)
such that
\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\mathcal{P}_{v}\preceq\mathcal{P}
. Obviously, it is also the case that
\xi\in\mathcal{D}(S^{\prime})
, since
S\subseteq S^{\prime}
, therefore we immediately have that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bignd_{X\in E^{\prime}}\varphi
.

(9)

\frac{\varphi\vdash\psi}{\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi\vdash\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\psi}

Suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi
and let
\nu=d(\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma)
. So,
\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}\mathcal{P}_{v}\preceq\mathcal{P}
such that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})\vDash\varphi
for each
v
. Since
\varphi\vdash\psi
, we get that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})\vDash\psi
for each
v
. Therefore,
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\psi
. Note that
\psi
may not witness a partition of the sample space, but the
\mathcal{P}_{v}
s are still disjoint.

(10)

\frac{Y\notin\mathsf{fv}(\varphi)}{\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi\vdash\bigoplus_{Y\sim d(E)}\varphi[Y/X]}

Suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi
, and let
\nu=d(\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma))
. This means that
\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}\mathcal{P}_{v}\preceq\mathcal{P}
such that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})\vDash\varphi
for each
v\in\mathsf{supp}(\nu)
. Since
Y\notin\mathsf{fv}(\varphi)
, then clearly
\Gamma[Y\coloneqq v],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})\vDash\varphi[Y/X]
. Therefore, we get that
\Gamma,\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}\mathcal{P}_{v}\vDash\bigoplus_{Y\sim d(E)}\varphi[Y/X]
, and since
\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}\mathcal{P}_{v}\preceq\mathcal{P}
, then
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bigoplus_{Y\sim d(E)}\varphi[Y/X]
by
Lemma

E.1
.

(11)

\frac{X\notin\mathsf{fv}(\psi)}{(\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi)\ast\psi\vdash\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}(\varphi\ast\psi)}

Suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash(\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi)\ast\psi
and let
\nu=d(\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma))
. So,
(\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}\mathcal{P}_{v})\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mathcal{P}
such that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})\vDash\varphi
for each
v
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\vDash\psi
. Since
X\notin\mathsf{fv}(\psi)
, then
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\vDash\psi
for each
v
. Also note that
\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\prime})
since
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
is already a complete probability space. This gives us
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\prime})\vDash\varphi\ast\psi
. Now, by
Lemma

B.3
, we have:

\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}(\mathcal{P}_{v}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\prime})=\left(\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}\mathcal{P}_{v}\right)\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mathcal{P}

Therefore,
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}(\varphi\ast\psi)
.

(12)

\frac{X\notin\mathsf{fv}(\psi)\hskip 8.24454pt\mathsf{precise}(\psi)}{\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}(\varphi\ast\psi)\vdash(\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi)\ast\psi}

Suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}(\varphi\ast\psi)
, and let
\nu=d(\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma))
. This means that
\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}(\mathcal{P}_{v}\otimes\mathcal{Q}_{v})\preceq\mathcal{P}
such that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})\vDash\varphi
and
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{Q}_{v})\vDash\psi
. Since
X\notin\mathsf{fv}(\psi)
, we also know that
\Gamma,\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{Q}_{v})\vDash\psi
, and since
\psi
is precise, there is a unique
\mathcal{Q}
such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi
and
\mathcal{Q}\preceq\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{Q}_{v})
for all
v
. Therefore, by recombining the components, we get that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash(\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi)\ast\psi
.

(13)

\frac{X\notin\mathsf{fv}(\psi)\hskip 8.24454pt\mathsf{convex}(\psi)}{\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}(\varphi\mathop{\ast_{\mathsf{w}}}\psi)\vdash(\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi)\mathop{\ast_{\mathsf{w}}}\psi}

Suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}(\varphi\mathop{\ast_{\mathsf{w}}}\psi)
, which means that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})\vDash\varphi\mathop{\ast_{\mathsf{w}}}\psi
for each
v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)
where
\xi=d(\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma))
and
\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\mathcal{P}_{v}\preceq\mathcal{P}
. Let
S
be the set such that
\Omega_{\mathcal{P}}=\mathsf{Mem}[S]
and
T=\mathsf{vars}(\psi)
, so
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\pi_{S\setminus T}(\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v}))\vDash\varphi
, and therefore
\Gamma,\pi_{S\setminus T}(\mathcal{P})\vDash\varphi
. In addition, we know that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\pi_{T}(\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v}))\vDash\psi
for each
v
, and since
\psi
is convex, then
\Gamma,\pi_{T}(\mathcal{P})\vDash\psi
(see
Item

14
for more details). Combining these two facts, we get that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash(\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi)\mathop{\ast_{\mathsf{w}}}\psi
.

(14)

\frac{X\notin\mathsf{fv}(\varphi)\hskip 8.24454pt\mathsf{convex}(\varphi)}{\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi\vdash\varphi}

Suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi
, and let
\nu=d(\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma))
. This means that
\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}\mathcal{P}_{v}\preceq\mathcal{P}
and
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})\vDash\varphi
. Since
X\notin\mathsf{fv}(\varphi)
,this also means that
\Gamma,\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})\vDash\varphi
. Since
\varphi
is convex, there exist
\Omega
,
\mathcal{F}
, and
S
and for each
v

\mu_{v}\in S
such that
\Gamma,\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu_{v}\rangle\vDash\varphi
and
\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu_{v}\rangle\preceq\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})
. Now, let
\mu=\sum_{x\in\mathsf{supp}(\nu)}\nu(v)\cdot\mu_{v}
, so clearly
\mu\in S
since it is a convex combination of elements of
S
, and therefore,
\Gamma,\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu\rangle\vDash\varphi
.
It remains only to show that
\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu\rangle\preceq\mathcal{P}
. The conditions on
\Omega
and
\mathcal{F}
hold trivially since
\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu_{v}\rangle\preceq\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})
for all
v
. Now, we show the condition on
\mu_{\mathcal{P}}
:

\displaystyle\mu(A)

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\nu)}\nu(v)\cdot\mu_{v}(A)

Let
U
be the set such that
\Omega=\mathsf{Mem}[U]
. Since
\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu_{v}\rangle\preceq\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})
.

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\nu)}\nu(v)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{v}}\left(\bigcup\left\{B\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}_{v}}\mid\pi_{U}(B)=A\right\}\right)

Since
\langle\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mu_{v}\rangle\preceq\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})
, then
\mu_{v}
gives measure 0 to all events outside of
\Omega_{\mathcal{P}_{v}}
, therefore we can add the following intersection.

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\nu)}\nu(v)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{v}}\left(\bigcup\left\{B\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}_{v}}\mid\pi_{U}(B)=A\right\}\cap\Omega_{\mathcal{P}_{v}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mu_{\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}\mathcal{P}_{v}}\left(\bigcup\left\{B\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}\mid\pi_{U}(B)=A\right\}\right)

Since
\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}\mathcal{P}_{v}\preceq\mathcal{P}
.

\displaystyle=\mu_{\mathcal{P}}\left(\bigcup\left\{B\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}\mid\pi_{U}(B)=A\right\}\right)

∎

E.3.
Soundness of Inference Rules

We start by providing a lemma stating that weak triples can be stated without the frame preservation property.

Lemma E.9 (Alternative Characterization of Weak Triples).

I\vDash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 17.00024pt\text{iff}\hskip 17.00024pt\forall\Gamma,\mu.\ \Gamma,\mu\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\implies\forall\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu).\ \Gamma,\nu\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil

Proof.

We show both directions:

(\Rightarrow)

Suppose that
\Gamma,\mu\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.
Let
\mathcal{P}_{F}
be the trivial probability space on
\mathsf{Mem}[\emptyset]
, so clearly
\mu\diamond_{\mathsf{w}}\mathcal{P}_{F}=\{\mu\}
.
Now take any
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
. Since
I\vDash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
, we get that there exists a
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}\diamond_{\mathsf{w}}\mathcal{P}_{F}
such that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\preceq\nu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Since
\mathcal{Q}\diamond_{\mathsf{w}}\mathcal{P}_{F}=\{\mathcal{Q}\}
, then
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}=\mathcal{Q}
, therefore
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. In addition, since
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\preceq\nu
, then by
Lemma

E.1
,
\Gamma,\nu\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.

(\Leftarrow)

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\diamond_{\mathsf{w}}\mathcal{P}_{F}
such that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.
Let
U\subseteq\mathsf{Var}
be the variables of
\mathcal{P}
and
I
and
V
be the variables of
\mathcal{P}_{F}
. Clearly
\Gamma,\pi_{U}(\mu)\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Since
\pi_{U}(\mu)\preceq\mu
, then by
Lemma

E.1
,
\Gamma,\mu\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
too. So, by the premise,
\Gamma,\nu\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
for any
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
. But
\psi
and
I
only depend on the variables
U
, so
\Gamma,\pi_{U}(\nu)\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
too. In addition, it must be the case that
\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\pi_{V}(\nu)
since
C
does not alter any variables in
V
. Now, let
\mathcal{Q}=\pi_{U}(\nu)
and construct
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
as follows:

\Omega_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}\triangleq\Omega_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\hskip 17.00024pt\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}\triangleq\sigma(\{A_{1}\ast A_{2}\mid A_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}},A_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}_{F}}\})\hskip 17.00024pt\mu_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}(A)=\nu(A)

So clearly by construction,
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}\diamond_{\mathsf{w}}\mathcal{P}_{F}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\preceq\nu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Therefore, we are done.

∎

\hypertarget{rule:Disj}{}\frac{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012ptI\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}\vee\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\textsc{Disj}}\par\hypertarget{rule:Exists2}{}\frac{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012ptX\notin\mathsf{vars}(\psi,I)}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists X.\ \varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\textsc{Exists2}}\par\hypertarget{rule:Subst}{}\frac{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{I[E/X]\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi[E/X]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi[E/X]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\textsc{Subst}}

Figure 12
.
Additional Inference Rules

See
5.2

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the derivation.

•

Skip
.

\frac{\;}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{skip}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Skip}{\textsc{Skip}}}

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
,
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mu
, and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. By
Zilberstein
et al. (2025a, Lemma 5.2)
, we have:

\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket\texttt{skip}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)=\eta^{\dagger}(\mu)=\{\mu\}

So, letting
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}=\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
, clearly
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\preceq\mu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.

•

Seq
.

\frac{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\vartheta{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012ptI\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\vartheta{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Seq}{\textsc{Seq}}}

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
,
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mu
, and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. By
Zilberstein
et al. (2025a, Lemma 5.2)
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket\fatsemi\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket^{\dagger}(\mu))

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\mu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)}\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu^{\prime})

So, for every
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\fatsemi C_{2}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
, there is a
\mu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
such that
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu^{\prime})
. By the induction hypotheses, we know there exist
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
such that
\mathcal{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\preceq\mu^{\prime}
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}_{1}\vDash\vartheta\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. By the induction hypothesis again, we get that there exist
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
such that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\preceq\nu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.

•

IfT
.

\frac{\varphi\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b\mapsto\mathsf{true}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\hskip 8.50012ptI\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:IfT}{\textsc{IfT}}}

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
,
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mu
, and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. We therefore know that
\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Test}}(\sigma)=\mathsf{true}
for all
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu)
. So, by
Zilberstein
et al. (2025a, Lemma 5.2)
, we get that:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\mathsf{guard}(b,\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket,\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket))^{\dagger}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\left(\lambda\sigma.\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket)(\sigma)&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Test}}(\sigma)=\mathsf{true}\\
\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)(\sigma)&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Test}}(\sigma)=\mathsf{false}\end{array}\right.\right)^{\dagger}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)

So any
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
must also be in
\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
, and therefore we can use the induction hypothesis to conclude that there is a
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
such that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\preceq\nu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.

•

IfF
. Symmetric to the
IfT
case.

•

Assign
.

\frac{\varphi\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e\mapsto E\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\land(\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathsf{own}(x)\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil)}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~x\coloneqq e~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x\mapsto E\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Assign}{\textsc{Assign}}}

We prove only the
m=\mathsf{s}
case, as the
m=\mathsf{w}
case follows from the soundness of the
Weakening
rule.
Suppose that
\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x\mapsto E\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Let
S
be the set of variables such that
\mu\in\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{Mem}[S])
.
Since
\varphi\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e\mapsto E\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, we know that
\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(\sigma)=\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma)
for all
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu)
. Now take any
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket x\coloneqq e\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
. We know from
Zilberstein
et al. (2025a, Lemma 5.2)
that:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket x\coloneqq e\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\boldsymbol{\langle}x\coloneqq e\boldsymbol{\rangle})^{\dagger}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\left(\left\llbracket x\coloneqq e\right\rrbracket^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}_{\mathsf{Act}}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu)

From the premise of the rule, we know that
x\coloneqq e
does not depend on
\mathsf{vars}(I)
, so the invariant sensitive semantics is the same as the regular semantics.

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket x\coloneqq e\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Act}}^{\dagger}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\left\{\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu)}\mu(\sigma)\cdot\nu_{\sigma}\;\;\Big|\;\;\forall\sigma.\ \nu_{\sigma}\in\left\llbracket x\coloneqq e\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Act}}(\sigma)\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu)}\mu(\sigma)\cdot\delta_{\sigma[x\coloneqq\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(\sigma)]}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu)}\mu(\sigma)\cdot\delta_{\sigma[x\coloneqq\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma)]}\right\}

Now, since
\varphi\Rightarrow\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{own}(x)\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, we get that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{own}(x)\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, so there exist
\mathcal{P}_{1}
,
\mathcal{P}_{2}
, and
\mathcal{P}_{3}
such that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{3}\preceq\mathcal{P}
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\vDash\psi
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{2}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{own}(x)\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{3}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Since
\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{own}(x)\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
is precise, let
\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}
be the unique smallest probability space such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{own}(x)\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, and note that
\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}\preceq\mathcal{P}_{2}
and
\Omega_{\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}}=\mathsf{Mem}[\{x\}]
. So, by
Lemma

B.4
, we have:

\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{3}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{3}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mu

From above, we know that
\nu={\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu)}}\mu(\sigma)\cdot\delta_{\sigma[x\coloneqq\left\llbracket E^{\prime}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma)]}
, so it is easy to see that
\pi_{S\setminus\{x\}}(\nu)=\pi_{S\setminus\{x\}}(\mu)
, therefore:

\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{3}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\pi_{S\setminus\{x\}}(\mu)=\pi_{S\setminus\{x\}}(\nu)

Let
\mathcal{Q}
be the trivial probability space that satisfies
\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x\mapsto E\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, so that
\Omega_{\mathcal{Q}}=\mathsf{Mem}[\{x\}]
all events in
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}
have probability 0 or 1, and
\mathcal{Q}\preceq\pi_{\{x\}}(\nu)
. So, clearly
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{3}\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x\mapsto E\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
and:
(\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{3})\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\nu
.

•

Samp
.

\frac{\varphi\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e\mapsto E\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\land(\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathsf{own}(x)\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~x\mathbin{:\approx}d(e)~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi\ast(x\sim d(E){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Samp}{\textsc{Samp}}}

We prove only the
m=\mathsf{s}
case, as the
m=\mathsf{w}
case follows from the soundness of the
Weakening
rule.
Suppose that
\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, where
\mu\in\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{Mem}[S])
.
Since
\varphi\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e\mapsto E\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, we know that
\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(\sigma)=\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma)
for all
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu)
. Now take any
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket x\mathbin{:\approx}d(e)\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
. We know that:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket x\mathbin{:\approx}d(e)\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\left(\left\llbracket x\mathbin{:\approx}d(e)\right\rrbracket^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}_{\mathsf{Act}}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu)

From the premise of the rule, we know that
x\mathbin{:\approx}e
does not depend on
\mathsf{vars}(I)
, so the invariant sensitive semantics is the same as the regular semantics.

\displaystyle=\left\llbracket x\mathbin{:\approx}d(e)\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Act}}^{\dagger}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\left\{\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu)}\mu(\sigma)\cdot\nu_{\sigma}\;\;\Big|\;\;\forall\sigma.\ \nu_{\sigma}\in\left\llbracket x\mathbin{:\approx}d(e)\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Act}}(\sigma)\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu)}\mu(\sigma)\cdot\sum_{v\in\mathsf{Val}}d(\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(\sigma))(v)\cdot\delta_{\sigma[x\coloneqq v]}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{{\sum_{v\in\mathsf{Val}}}d(\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma))(v)\cdot\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu)}\mu(\sigma)\cdot\delta_{\sigma[x\coloneqq v]}\right\}

Let
\nu_{v}={\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu)}}\mu(\sigma)\cdot\delta_{\sigma[x\coloneqq v]}
, therefore
\nu=\sum_{v\in\mathsf{Val}}d(\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma))(v)\cdot\nu_{v}
. Note also that
\pi_{S\setminus\{x\}}(\mu)=\pi_{S\setminus\{x\}}(\nu)
.
Since
\varphi\Rightarrow\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{own}(x)\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, we know that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{own}(x)\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, so there exist
\mathcal{P}_{1}
,
\mathcal{P}_{2}
, and
\mathcal{P}_{3}
such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\vDash\psi
,
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{2}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{own}(x)\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{3}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. This gives us:

\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{3}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\pi_{S\setminus\{x\}}(\mu)=\pi_{S\setminus\{x\}}(\nu)=\pi_{S\setminus\{x\}}(\nu_{v})

Now, let
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
be the trivial probability space such that
\Omega_{\mathcal{Q}_{v}}=\{\sigma\in\mathsf{Mem}[\{x\}]\mid\sigma(x)=v\}
and
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathcal{Q}_{v}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x\mapsto X\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. By Construction:

\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{Q}_{v})\otimes\mathcal{P}_{3}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}=(\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{3}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F})\otimes\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{Q}_{v})\preceq\pi_{S\setminus\{x\}}(\nu_{v})\otimes\pi_{\{x\}}(\nu_{v})=\nu_{v}

Let
\xi=d(\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma))
and
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes(\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\mathcal{Q}_{v})\otimes\mathcal{P}_{3}
, then using
Lemmas

B.5
and
B.3
we get:

\mathcal{Q}\otimes P_{F}=\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\left(\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\mathcal{Q}_{v}\right)\otimes\mathcal{P}_{3}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}=\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{Q}_{v}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{3}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\xi(v)\cdot\nu_{v}=\nu

By construction, we also have
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi\ast(x\sim d(E))\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.

Par
.

\frac{I\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012ptI\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012pt\mathsf{precise}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})}{I\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\ast\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}\parallel C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}\ast\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Par}{\textsc{Par}}}

Since we are using a strong triple, suppose that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mu
such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\vDash\varphi_{1}
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{2}\vDash\varphi_{2}
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{I}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.
Without loss of generality, suppose that
\mathcal{P}_{I}
is minimal.
To complete the proof, we need to establish the premise of
Lemma

C.6
.
Since
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
are precise, let
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
be the unique smallest probability spaces that satisfy them under
\Gamma
(note that if
\psi_{1}
or
\psi_{2}
is unsatisfiable, then the premise of the rule is false, so the claim holds vacuously).
Take any
\mu_{1}
such that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\preceq\mu_{1}
and any
\nu_{1}\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu_{1})
. By the premise of the
Par
rule, we know that there is a
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{1}\preceq\nu_{1}
such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{1}\vDash\psi_{1}\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, therefore
\mathcal{Q}_{1}\ast\mathcal{P}_{I}\preceq\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{1}
, and therefore we have shown that:

\forall\mu_{1}.\ \mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\preceq\mu_{1}\implies\forall\nu_{1}\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu_{1}).\ \mathcal{Q}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\preceq\nu_{1}

We now perform a nearly identical argument for
C_{2}
, but we also handle the frame. Take any
\mu_{2}
such that
\mathcal{P}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mu_{2}
and any
\nu_{2}\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu_{2})
. By the second premise of the
Par
rule, we get that there is a
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}
such that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\nu_{2}
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}\vDash\psi_{2}\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Due to precision, we know that
\mathcal{Q}_{2}\ast\mathcal{P}_{I}\preceq\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}
and so by
Lemma

B.4
,
\mathcal{Q}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\nu_{2}
, so we have shown that:

\forall\mu_{2}.\ \mathcal{P}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mu_{2}\implies\forall\nu_{2}.\ \mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu_{2}).\ \mathcal{Q}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\nu_{2}

Now, by
Lemma

C.6
,
\mathcal{Q}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{Q}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\nu
for all
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket\parallel\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
. Let
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{Q}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{Q}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}
. So
\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\nu
and since
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}_{1}\vDash\psi_{1}
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}_{2}\vDash\psi_{2}
, we get that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi_{1}\ast\psi_{2}\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, so we are done.
