Atom
.

\frac{J\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~a~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{{I\ast J}\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~a~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Atom}{\textsc{Atom}}}

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
,
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mu
, and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\ast J\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. This means that there exist
\mathcal{P}_{1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}
such that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{3}\preceq\mathcal{P}
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\vDash\varphi
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{2}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\ast J\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{I}
and
\mathcal{P}_{J}
be the trivial probability spaces that satisfy
\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
and
\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}J\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, respectively, so clearly
\mathcal{P}_{I}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{J}\preceq\mathcal{P}_{2}
.
Now, take any
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\ast J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket a\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
and note that:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\ast J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket a\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)

\displaystyle=(\left\llbracket a\right\rrbracket^{\llparenthesis I\ast J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}_{\mathsf{Act}})^{\dagger}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\left(\left(\mathsf{check}^{\llparenthesis I\ast J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}\right)^{\dagger}\circ\left\llbracket a\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Act}}^{\dagger}\circ\left(\mathsf{replace}^{\llparenthesis I\ast J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}\right)^{\dagger}\circ\left(\mathsf{check}^{\llparenthesis I\ast J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}\right)\right)^{\dagger}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathsf{check}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}\ast\llparenthesis J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\left\llbracket a\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Act}}^{\dagger}\left(\left(\mathsf{replace}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}\ast\llparenthesis J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\left(\mathsf{check}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}\ast\llparenthesis J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu)\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathsf{check}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket a\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}\left(\left(\mathsf{replace}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\left(\mathsf{check}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu)\right)\right)\right)

Since we already assumed that
\mu
satisfies
I\ast J
, the first check does nothing.

\displaystyle=\left(\mathsf{check}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket a\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}\left(\left(\mathsf{replace}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}\right)^{\dagger}(\mu)\right)\right)

Therefore, we know that
\nu=(\mathsf{check}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}})^{\dagger}(\nu^{\prime})
for some
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket a\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu^{\prime})
and
\mu^{\prime}\in(\mathsf{replace}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}})^{\dagger}(\mu))
.
We know that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{J}\preceq\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}\preceq\mathcal{P}
, so there must be a
\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\in(\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{J})\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
such that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\preceq\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
and therefore
\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\preceq\mu
. Since
\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}
only contains trivial information about
I
, and
\mu
and
\mu^{\prime}
differ only in the states that satisfy
I
, then
\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\preceq\mu^{\prime}
too.
Therefore, by the induction hypothesis, there must be
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
such that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\preceq\nu^{\prime}
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}J\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. This implies that
\Gamma,\nu^{\prime}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, and therefore
\nu=\nu^{\prime}
, since the check operation does nothing. Therefore, we get that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\preceq\nu^{\prime}=\nu
, and so we are done.

Share
.

\frac{I\ast J\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012pt\mathsf{finitary}(I)}{J\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Share}{\textsc{Share}}}

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
,
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mu
, and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}J\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Now, take any
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
. By
Lemma

5.3
, we know that:

\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\ast J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)=\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}\ast\llparenthesis J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)

And since
\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{C}}
is equivalent to
\supseteq
, then
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\ast J\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
as well. Therefore, by the induction hypothesis, we know that there exist
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
such that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\preceq\nu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\ast J\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Since
\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\ast J\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\Leftrightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}J\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, we are done.

•

Frame
.

\frac{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi\ast_{m}\vartheta{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi\ast_{m}\vartheta{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Frame}{\textsc{Frame}}}

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash(\varphi\ast_{m}\vartheta)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.
That means that there exist
\mathcal{P}_{1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{I}
such that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\preceq\mathcal{P}
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\vDash\varphi\ast_{m}\vartheta
, and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{I}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, and without loss of generality, suppose that
\mathcal{P}_{I}
is the trivial probability space that satisfies
I
. We also know that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{3}
where
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{2}\vDash\varphi
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{3}\vDash\vartheta
. Since
\mathcal{P}_{I}
is a trivial probability space, then
\mathcal{P}_{2}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{I}=\{\mathcal{P}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\}
, so by associativity of projections, we get that there must be a
\mathcal{P}_{F}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{F}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{3}
such that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{P}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I})\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}^{\prime}
.

Now, take any
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
. By the induction hypothesis, there exist
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\preceq\nu
and
\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. So, there must be a
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{3}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
, and by constructions
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}\vDash(\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil)\ast_{m}\vartheta
. Since
\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
is a pure assertion, this also means that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}\vDash(\psi\ast_{m}\vartheta)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.

•

Weaken
.

\frac{I\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{I\vdash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Weaken}{\textsc{Weaken}}}

Immediate from the induction hypothesis, letting
\mathcal{P}_{F}
be the trivial probability space on
\mathsf{Mem}[\emptyset]
, and
Lemma

E.9
.

•

Strengthen
.

\frac{I\vdash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012pt\mathsf{precise}(\psi)}{I\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Strengthen}{\textsc{Strengthen}}}

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mu
, and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.
This means that there exists a
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\vDash\varphi
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{I}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
where
\mathcal{P}_{I}
is the trivial probability space satisfying
\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\preceq\mathcal{P}
.
Since
\psi
is precise, let
\mathcal{Q}
be the unique smallest probability space such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi
. From the premise of the rule, we know that:

\forall\mu_{1}.\ \mathcal{P}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\preceq\mu_{1}\implies\forall\nu_{1}\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu_{1}).\ \mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\preceq\nu_{1}

In addition, since
\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket\texttt{skip}\right\rrbracket)=\eta

(
Zilberstein
et al., 2025a
, Lemma 5.2)
it is obvious that:

\forall\mu_{2}.\ \mathcal{P}_{F}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\preceq\mu_{2}\implies\forall\nu_{2}\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket\texttt{skip}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu_{2}).\ \mathcal{P}_{F}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\preceq\nu_{2}

So, by
Lemma

C.6
, we get that
\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\preceq\nu
for any
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket\parallel\left\llbracket\texttt{skip}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
. But clearly
\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket\parallel\left\llbracket\texttt{skip}\right\rrbracket)=\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)
, so we are done.

•

Split1
.

\frac{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012pt\psi\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e\mapsto X\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\hskip 8.50012ptX\notin\mathsf{fv}(I)}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Split1}{\textsc{Split1}}}

We first prove the claim for the case where
m=\mathsf{s}
.
Suppose that
\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash(\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. This means that
(\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\mathcal{P}_{v})\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\preceq\mathcal{P}
such that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})\vDash\varphi
for each
v
and
\xi=d(\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma))
and
\mathcal{P}_{I}
is the trivial probability space satisfying
\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. By
Lemmas

B.3
and
B.4
we have:

\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}(\mathcal{P}_{v}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F})=\left(\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\mathcal{P}_{v}\right)\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mu

Now, for each
v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)
, let
\mu_{v}(\sigma)\triangleq\frac{1}{\xi(v)}\cdot\mu(\sigma)
if
\sigma\in\Omega_{\mathcal{P}_{v}}\ast\Omega_{\mathcal{P}_{I}}\ast\Omega_{\mathcal{P}_{F}}
, which clearly gives us
\mathcal{P}_{v}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mu_{v}
and
\mu=\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\xi(v)\cdot\mu_{v}
. Now, take any
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
. We know that:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\nu(v)\cdot\mu_{v})

\displaystyle=\left\{\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\xi(v)\cdot\nu_{v}\;\;\Big|\;\;\forall v.\ \nu_{v}\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu_{v})\right\}

So
\nu=\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\xi(v)\cdot\nu_{v}
where
\nu_{v}\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu_{v})
for each
v
.
Note that
X\notin\mathsf{fv}(I)
, so
\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}=\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma[X\coloneqq v]}
, and therefore by the induction hypothesis, we get that there exists
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
such that
\mathcal{Q}_{v}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\nu_{v}
and
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathcal{Q}_{v}\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Since
\psi\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e=X\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, we can restrict the
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
s to disjoint probability spaces
\mathcal{Q}_{v}^{\prime}
such that
\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{Q}_{v}^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\preceq\mathcal{Q}_{v}
.

Therefore
\bigoplus_{v\sim\nu}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{v}
exists and
\Gamma,\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{v}\vDash\bigoplus_{v\sim d(E)}\psi
.
So, we get that:

\left(\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{v}\right)\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}=\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\left(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{v}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\right)\preceq\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\xi(v)\cdot\nu_{v}=\nu

The case where
m=\mathsf{w}
follows immediately from
Lemma

E.9
using the proof above with
\mathcal{P}_{F}
being the trivial probability space on empty memories.

NSplit1
.

\frac{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012pt\psi\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e\mapsto X\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\hskip 8.50012ptX\notin\mathsf{fv}(I)}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\bignd_{X\in E}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\bignd_{X\in E}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:NSplit1}{\textsc{NSplit1}}}

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash(\bignd_{X\in E}\varphi)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. This means that there is some
\xi\in\mathcal{D}(\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma))
such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash(\bigoplus_{X\sim\xi}\varphi)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.
Now take any
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
.
Using the
Split1
rule, we get that there exists a
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
such that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\preceq\nu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash(\bigoplus_{X\sim\xi}\psi)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. This immediately implies that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash(\bignd_{X\in E}\psi)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.

Split2
.

\frac{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012pt\mathsf{convex}(\psi)\hskip 8.50012ptX\notin\mathsf{vars}(I,\psi)}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Split2}{\textsc{Split2}}}

We start with the case where
m=\mathsf{s}
.
Suppose that
\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash(\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
and let
\xi=d(\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma))
. Since
X\notin\mathsf{vars}(I)
, then
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\bigoplus_{X\sim d(E)}\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
and so there exists a family of
\mathcal{P}_{v}
such that
\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\mathcal{P}_{v}\preceq\mathcal{P}
and
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
for all
v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)
. By
Lemmas

B.5
and
B.3
, we also have that:

\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\left(\mathcal{P}_{v}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\right)=\left(\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\mathcal{P}_{v}\right)\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mu

Now, for each
v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)
, let
\mu_{v}(\sigma)\triangleq\frac{1}{\xi(v)}\cdot\mu(\sigma)
if
\sigma\in\Omega_{\mathcal{P}_{v}}
, which clearly gives us
\mathsf{ext}(\mathcal{P}_{v})\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\mu_{v}
and
\mu=\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\xi(v)\cdot\mu_{v}
. Now, take any
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
. We know that:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\nu(v)\cdot\mu_{v})

\displaystyle=\left\{\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\xi(v)\cdot\nu_{v}\;\;\Big|\;\;\forall v.\ \nu_{v}\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu_{v})\right\}

So
\nu=\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\xi(v)\cdot\nu_{v}
where
\nu_{v}\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu_{v})
for each
v
.
Note that
X\notin\mathsf{vars}(I)
, so
\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}=\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma[X\coloneqq v]}
, and therefore by the induction hypothesis, we get that there exist a family of
\mathcal{Q}_{v}
such
\mathcal{Q}_{v}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\nu_{v}
and
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathcal{Q}_{v}\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Since
X\notin\mathsf{vars}(\psi)
, we can remove the update of
X
to conclude that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}_{v}\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.
Since
\psi
is convex, then we can presume that there exists
U
,
\mathcal{F}
, and
S
such that
\mathcal{Q}_{v}=\langle\mathsf{Mem}[U],\mathcal{F},\xi_{v}\rangle
for some
\xi_{v}\in S
. Now, let
\mathcal{Q}=\langle\mathsf{Mem}[U],\mathcal{F},\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\xi(v)\cdot\xi_{v}\rangle
. Since
\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\xi(v)\cdot\xi_{v}
is a convex combinations of elements of
S
, then
\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\xi(v)\cdot\xi_{v}\in S
and therefore by construction
\Gamma,Q\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. We now complete the proof by showing that
\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\nu
. Take any
A\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}
and
B\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}_{F}}
, and let
V
be the set such that
\Omega_{\mathcal{P}_{F}}=\mathsf{Mem}[V]
:

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}}(A\ast B)

\displaystyle=\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(A)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{F}}(B)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\xi(v)\cdot\xi_{v}(A)\right)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{F}}(B)

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\xi(v)\cdot\xi_{v}(A)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{F}}(B)

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\xi(v)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}_{v}}(A)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{F}}(B)

Since
\mathcal{Q}_{v}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{F}\preceq\nu_{v}
:

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)}\xi(v)\cdot\pi_{U\cup V}(\nu_{v})(A\ast B)

\displaystyle=\pi_{U\cup V}(\nu)(A)

The case where
m=\mathsf{w}
follows from
Lemma

E.9
using the same reasoning as above, but where
\mathcal{P}_{F}
is the trivial probability space on
\mathsf{Mem}[\emptyset]
.

NSplit2
.

\frac{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012pt\mathsf{convex}(\psi)\hskip 8.50012ptX\notin\mathsf{fv}(I,\psi)}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\bignd_{X\in E}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:NSplit2}{\textsc{NSplit2}}}

\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}

\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash(\bignd_{X\in E}\varphi)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil

\xi\in\mathcal{D}(\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma))

\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash(\bigoplus_{X\sim\xi}\varphi)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil

Exists
.

\frac{I\vdash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\textstyle\bignd_{X\in E}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012pt\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}e\mapsto X\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil}{I\vdash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\exists X\in E.\ P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Exists}{\textsc{Exists}}}

By
Lemma

E.9
, it suffices to show that the claim holds without frame preservation.
Suppose that
\Gamma,\mu\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\exists X\in E.P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Without loss of generality, suppose that
X
is not free in
I
(if not, then we could
\alpha
-rename
X
in
P
to obtain an equivalent assertion,
i.e.,

\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\exists X\in E.\ P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\Leftrightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\exists Y\in E.\ P[Y/X]\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
where
Y
is some fresh variable). So, we get that
\Gamma,\mu\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\exists X\in E.\ P\ast I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, which means that for every
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu)
there exists
v_{\sigma}\in\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma)
such that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v_{\sigma}],\sigma\vDash P\ast I
.
Let
\xi\triangleq\sum_{\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu)}\mu(\sigma)\cdot\delta_{v_{\sigma}}
, so
\xi(v)
is the probability that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v]
is the context that satisfies
P
. Also, let:

\mu_{v}(\sigma)\triangleq\frac{1}{\xi(v)}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mu(\sigma)&\text{if}~v=v_{\sigma}\\
0&\text{if}~v\neq v_{\sigma}\end{array}\right.

Clearly, by construction
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mu_{v}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
for all
v\in\mathsf{supp}(\xi)
.
Also, since
\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}e\mapsto X\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, then
\left\llbracket e\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{Exp}}(\sigma)=v
for each
\sigma\in\mathsf{supp}(\mu_{v})
, and so each
\mu_{v}=\mathsf{ext}(\mu_{v}^{\prime})
where
\mu_{v}^{\prime}
has a restricted sample space to only be over states where
e
evaluates to
v
. Thus, we clearly have
\bigoplus_{v\sim\xi}\mu^{\prime}_{v}\preceq\mu
, and therefore
\Gamma,\mu\vDash(\bignd_{X\in E}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.
So, by the induction hypothesis and
Lemma

E.9
,
\Gamma,\nu\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
for all
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
.

•

Consequence
.

\frac{\varphi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\varphi\hskip 8.50012ptI\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012pt\psi\Rightarrow\psi^{\prime}}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Consequence}{\textsc{Consequence}}}

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}})\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
,
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mu
, and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi^{\prime}
.
Since
\varphi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\varphi
, then
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi
. By the induction hypothesis, there exists
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}})\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
such that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\preceq\nu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi
for any
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
. Since
\psi\Rightarrow\psi^{\prime}
, then
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi^{\prime}
, so we are done.

•

BoundedRank
.
Subject to the following conditions:

\displaystyle(1)\

\displaystyle\varphi\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ell\leq R\leq h\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil

\displaystyle(2)\

\displaystyle\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R=\ell\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}b\mapsto\mathsf{false}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil

\displaystyle(3)\

\displaystyle\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R>\ell\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}b\mapsto\mathsf{true}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil

\displaystyle(4)\

\displaystyle\mathsf{precise}(\varphi[\ell/R])

\displaystyle(5)\

\displaystyle N\notin\mathsf{vars}(\varphi)

\displaystyle(6)\

\displaystyle 0<p\leq 1

The following inference is valid:

\frac{\textstyle I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R=N>\ell\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}(\bignd_{R=\ell}^{N-1}\varphi)\oplus_{\geq p}(\bignd_{R=N}^{h}\varphi){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\textstyle I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\bignd_{R=\ell}^{h}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi[\ell/R]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:BoundedRank}{\textsc{BoundedRank}}}

We prove the rule without frame preservation, as frame preservation follows from
Lemma

E.9
if
m=\mathsf{w}
and the
Strengthen
rule if
m=\mathsf{s}
(since the postcondition is precise).
Suppose that
\Gamma,\mu\vDash\bignd_{R=\ell}^{h}\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.
Let
\varphi^{\prime}=\bignd_{R=\ell+1}^{h}\varphi
and
\psi=\varphi[\ell/R]
. We first show that
\langle\varphi^{\prime},\psi\rangle
is an invariant pair for
\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C
under
I
:

(1)

\varphi^{\prime}=\bignd_{R=\ell+1}^{h}\varphi\Rightarrow\bignd_{R=\ell+1}^{h}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b\mapsto\mathsf{true}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}b\mapsto\mathsf{true}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil

(2)

\psi=\varphi[\ell/R]\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b\mapsto\mathsf{false}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil

(3)

We can weaken the postcondition of the premise of rule as follows:

(\bignd_{R=\ell}^{N-1}\varphi)\oplus_{\geq p}(\bignd_{R=N}^{h}\varphi)\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;(\bignd_{R=\ell}^{N-1}\varphi)\mathbin{\&}(\bignd_{R=N}^{h}\varphi)\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;\bignd_{R=\ell}^{h}\varphi\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;\varphi^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\psi

So, after an application of
NSplit2
, we get that
I\vDash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
. If
m=\mathsf{s}
,
Weaken
can be used to obtain
I\vDash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi^{\prime}\mathbin{\&}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}
.

(4)

By assumption, we have
\mathsf{precise}(\psi)
.

In addition, the premise of the rule implies that each iteration, the rank decreases by at least 1 with probability at least
p
, so starting in any state satisfying
\varphi
, the loop will terminate with probability at least
p^{h-\ell}>0
. Therefore by
Corollary

D.3
,
\mathsf{minterm}(\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu))=1
,
i.e.,
the loop almost surely terminates.

Since
\varphi[\ell/R]
is precise, let
\mathcal{Q}
be the unique smallest probability space such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\varphi[\ell/R]
. Take any event
B\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}
. By
Lemma

D.2
, we get:

\mathsf{minProb}\left(\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu),B\ast\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}\right)=\mathsf{minterm}\left(\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)\right)\cdot\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(B)=\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(B)

So,
\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(B)\leq\nu(B\ast\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma})
for any
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket\texttt{while}~b~\texttt{do}~C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
. However, we already established that the loop almost surely terminates, so
\nu(\bot)=0
, therefore
\nu(B\ast\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma})=1-\nu(\mathsf{Mem}[V]\setminus(B\ast\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}))
(where
V
is the domain of
\nu
), which gives us:

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(B)

\displaystyle\leq\nu(B\ast\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma})

\displaystyle=1-\nu(\mathsf{Mem}[V]\setminus(B\ast\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}))

\displaystyle\leq 1-\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(\mathsf{Mem}[V]\setminus(B\ast\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}))

\displaystyle=1-(1-\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(B\ast\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}))

\displaystyle=\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(B)

Therefore
\mu_{\mathcal{Q}}(B)=\nu(B\ast\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma})
, and since this is true for any
B\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}
, then
\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{I}\preceq\nu
where
\mathcal{P}_{I}
is the trivial probability space satisfying
I
. By definition,
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\varphi[\ell/R]
, so we are done.

•

Disj
.

\frac{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012ptI\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi_{1}\vee\psi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Disj}{\textsc{Disj}}}

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\diamond\mathcal{P}_{F}
and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash(\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2})\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. This means that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}\preceq\mathcal{P}
such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\vDash\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2}
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{2}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Without loss of generality, suppose that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\vDash\varphi_{1}
, therefore
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi_{1}\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Now take any
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
. By the induction hypothesis, we know that there exists
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
such that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\preceq\nu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi_{1}\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. We can weaken this to conclude that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash(\psi_{1}\vee\psi_{2})\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.

•

Exists2
.

\frac{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012ptX\notin\mathsf{vars}(\psi,I)}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\exists X.\ \varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Exists2}{\textsc{Exists2}}}

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\diamond\mathcal{P}_{F}
and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash(\exists X.\varphi)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. This means that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2}\preceq\mathcal{P}
such that
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{1}\vDash\exists X.\varphi
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}_{2}\vDash\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.
Therefore,
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathcal{P}_{1}\vDash\varphi
for some
v\in\mathsf{Val}
. Since
X\notin\mathsf{vars}(I)
, then we also have that
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.
Now take any
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
.
Since
I\notin\mathsf{vars}(I)
, then
\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma[X\coloneqq v]}=\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}
, so
\nu\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma[X\coloneqq v]}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
as well.
By the induction hypothesis, we know that there exists
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
such that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\preceq\nu
and
\Gamma[X\coloneqq v],\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Since
X\notin\mathsf{vars}(\psi,I)
, then this implies that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
.

Subst

\frac{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{I[E/X]\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi[E/X]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi[E/X]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\hyperlink{rule:Subst}{\textsc{Subst}}}

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
such that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\preceq\mu
and
\Gamma,\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi[E/X]\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I[E/X]\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
. Let
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma[X\coloneqq\left\llbracket E\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{LExp}}(\Gamma)]
, so by construction
\Gamma^{\prime},\mathcal{P}\vDash\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil
, and
\llparenthesis I[E/X]\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}=\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
. Now, take any
\nu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I[E/X]\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)=\mathcal{L}^{\llparenthesis I\rrparenthesis_{\Gamma^{\prime}}}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}(\mu)
. By the induction hypothesis, we know that there exist
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}\diamond_{m}\mathcal{P}_{F}
such that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\preceq\nu
and
\Gamma^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi
. This means that
\Gamma,\mathcal{Q}\vDash\psi[E/X]
.

∎

E.4.
Derived Rules

Lemma E.10.

The following inference rule is derivable:

\hypertarget{rule:If}{}\frac{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X=\mathsf{true}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012ptI\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}X=\mathsf{false}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012pt\varphi\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}b\mapsto X\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\hskip 8.50012pt\psi\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}b\mapsto X\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\textstyle\bigoplus_{X\sim\mathbf{Ber}\left(p\right)}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\bigoplus_{X\sim\mathbf{Ber}\left(p\right)}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\textsc{If}}

Proof.

Note in the proof below that
\mathsf{true}=1
and
\mathsf{false}=0
.

\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X=1\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X=1\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\enskip\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:IfT}{\footnotesize{IfT}}}\frac{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X=0\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X=0\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\enskip\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:IfF}{\footnotesize{IfF}},vdots=4.5em,leftskip=5em,rightskip=5em}}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}(\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X=1\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil)\vee(\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}X=0\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi\vee\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.24454pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Disj}{\footnotesize{Disj}}}}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\textstyle\bigoplus_{X\sim\mathbf{Ber}\left(p\right)}(\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X=1\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil)\vee(\varphi\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}X=0\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\bigoplus_{X\sim\mathbf{Ber}\left(p\right)}\psi\vee\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Split1}{\footnotesize{Split1}}}}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\textstyle\bigoplus_{X\sim\mathbf{Ber}\left(p\right)}\varphi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\bigoplus_{X\sim\mathbf{Ber}\left(p\right)}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Consequence}{\footnotesize{Consequence}}}

∎

Lemma E.11.

The following inference rule is derivable:

\hypertarget{rule:IfPure}{}\frac{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\mapsto 1\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012ptI\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\land b\mapsto 0\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012pt\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}b\in\{0,1\}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil}{I\vdash_{m}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\textsc{IfPure}}

Proof.

We only show the derivation of the weak version. The strong version can be easily derived using
Strengthen
, since the postcondition is precise (
e.g.,
see
Lemma

E.12
). Let
X
be some fresh logical variable such that
X\notin\mathsf{vars}(P,I)
.

\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{I\vdash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\mapsto 1\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{1}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{I\vdash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\mapsto 1\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\enskip\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:IfT}{\footnotesize{IfT}}}\frac{I\vdash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\mapsto 0\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{I\vdash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\mapsto 0\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\enskip\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:IfF}{\footnotesize{IfF}},vdots=4.5em,leftskip=4em,rightskip=8em}}{I\vdash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\mapsto X\land(X=0\vee X=1)\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\enskip\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Disj}{\footnotesize{Disj}}}}{I\vdash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\textstyle\bignd_{X\in\{0,1\}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\land b\mapsto X\land(X=0\vee X=1)\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.24454pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:NSplit2}{\footnotesize{NSplit2}}}}{I\vdash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\exists X.\ P\land b\mapsto X\land(X=0\vee X=1)\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Exists}{\footnotesize{Exists}}}}{I\vdash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~C_{1}~\texttt{else}~C_{2}~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}Q\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Consequence}{\footnotesize{Consequence}}}

∎

Lemma E.12.

The following inference rule is derivable:

\hypertarget{rule:ExistsStrong}{}\frac{I\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\textstyle\bignd_{X\in E}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}\hskip 8.50012pt\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}e\mapsto X\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\hskip 8.50012pt\mathsf{precise}(\psi)}{I\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\exists X\in E.\ P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{\textsc{ExistsStrong}}

Proof.

\frac{\frac{\frac{I\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\textstyle\bignd_{X\in E}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}{I\vdash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\textstyle\bignd_{X\in E}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.24454pt\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Weaken}{\footnotesize{Weaken}}}\hskip 8.24454pt\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\Rightarrow\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}e\mapsto X\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil}{I\vdash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\exists X\in E.\ P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Exists}{\footnotesize{Exists}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\mathsf{precise}(\psi)}{I\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\exists X\in E.\ P\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~C~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\psi{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Strengthen}{\footnotesize{Strengthen}}}

∎

Appendix F
Examples

In this section, we provide derivations that were omitted in the main text. The content is rotated to take advantage of the full width of the page.

F.1.
Conditional Independence Example

In this section, we show the derivation for the program that was introduced in
Section

2
. The program is repeated below:

z\mathbin{:\approx}\mathbf{Ber}\left(\tfrac{1}{2}\right)\fatsemi\left(x\coloneqq z\;\parallel\;y\coloneqq 1-z\right)

The first step is to break up the sequential composition, and derive a specification for the sampling operation. This is simple using the
Seq
,
Frame
, and
Samp
rules. However, the derivation for the second part of the program is more difficult, and will be filled in shortly where the
(\bigstar)
appears.

\frac{\frac{\frac{\;}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{own}(z){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~z\mathbin{:\approx}\mathbf{Ber}\left(1/2\right)~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}z\sim\mathbf{Ber}\left(1/2\right){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.24454pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Samp}{{Samp}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{own}(x,y,z){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~z\mathbin{:\approx}\mathbf{Ber}\left(1/2\right)~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{own}(x,y)\ast z\sim\mathbf{Ber}\left(1/2\right){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Frame}{{Frame}}}\hskip 8.50012pt(\bigstar)}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{own}(x,y,z){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~z\mathbin{:\approx}\mathbf{Ber}\left(1/2\right)\fatsemi(x\coloneqq z\parallel y\coloneqq 1-z)~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\textstyle\bigoplus_{\mathbf{Ber}\left(1/2\right)}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x\mapsto Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}y\mapsto 1-Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}z\mapsto Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Seq}{{Seq}}}

We now show the
(\bigstar)
derivation. The first step is to rearrange the precondition using the entailment laws from
Figure

4
to bring the outcome conjunction to the outside, so that we can apply the
Cond1
rule. Conditioning makes
z
deterministic, and therefore trivially independent from the rest of the state, so we can allocate an invariant with the
Share
rule. The rest of the proof is straightforward.

\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\;}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{own}(x)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z\mapsto Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~x\coloneqq z~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}x\mapsto Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}z\mapsto Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\enskip\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Assign}{{Assign}}}}{z\mapsto Z\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{own}(x){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~x\coloneqq z~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x\mapsto Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\enskip\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Atom}{{Atom}}}\quad\frac{\frac{\;}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{own}(y)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z\mapsto Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~y\coloneqq 1-z~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}y\mapsto 1-Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}z\mapsto Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\enskip\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Assign}{{Assign}}}}{z\mapsto Z\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{own}(y){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~y\coloneqq 1-z~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y\mapsto 1-Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\enskip\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Atom}{{Atom}}}}{z\mapsto Z\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{own}(x,y){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~x\coloneqq z\parallel y\coloneqq 1-z~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x\mapsto Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}y\mapsto 1-Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\enskip\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Par}{{Par}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{own}(x,y)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z\mapsto Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~x\coloneqq z\parallel y\coloneqq 1-z~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}x\mapsto Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}y\mapsto 1-Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}z\mapsto Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.24454pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Share}{{Share}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\textstyle\bigoplus_{\mathbf{Ber}\left(1/2\right)}\mathsf{own}(x,y)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}z\mapsto 1-z~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\textstyle\bigoplus_{\mathbf{Ber}\left(1/2\right)}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}x\mapsto Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Right=\hyperlink{rule:Cond1}{{Cond1}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\mathsf{own}(x,y)\ast z\sim\mathbf{Ber}\left(1/2\right){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~x\coloneqq z\parallel y\coloneqq 1-z~{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\textstyle\bigoplus_{\mathbf{Ber}\left(1/2\right)}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x\mapsto Z\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{right=\hyperlink{rule:Consequence}{{Consequence}}}

F.2.

Recall the following random walk program from

.

\texttt{while}~x>0~\texttt{do}~b\mathbin{:\approx}\textstyle\mathbf{Ber}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)\fatsemi\texttt{if}~b~\texttt{then}~x\coloneqq x-1~\texttt{else}~x\coloneqq y

F.3.
Entropy Mixer

Recall the following entropy mixer program from
Section

6.1
.

y\coloneqq 0\fatsemi\left(\;\;x_{1}\coloneqq y\fatsemi x_{2}\mathbin{:\approx}\mathbf{Ber}\left(\tfrac{1}{2}\right)\fatsemi z\coloneqq\xor(x_{1},x_{2})\;\;{\parallel}\;\;y\coloneqq 1\;\;\right)

We will analyze this program using the invariant
y\in\{0,1\}
, and conclude in the end that
z\sim\mathbf{Ber}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)
. We begin by analyzing the read from shared state in the first thread. The derivation is given below in (
9
).

(9)

\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\frac{\;}{\vdash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}y\mapsto Y\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil)\ast\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}y\in\{0,1\}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Right={\hyperlink{rule:Exists}{\footnotesize{Exists}}}}}{y\in\{0,1\}\vdash_{\mathsf{w}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{own}(x_{1})\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\rceil{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\rangle}}~x_{1}\coloneqq First,
Atom
is applied to open the invariant. Next, we use
Exists
and
NSplit1
to gain access to the value of
y
, so that we can apply the
Assign
rule. The rule of
Consequence
is used to weaken the information about
y
and move it out of the scope of the
\bignd
, so that the invariant can be closed. Since the postcondition at this stage is not precise, we are forced to use a weak triple. We now move on to derive a specification for the write to
z
below in (
10
).

(10)

\frac{\frac{\frac{}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\boldsymbol{\langle}}\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\lceil\color[rgb]{0,0,0}x_{1}\mapsto
